{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeNMmNV771eyWyATY28tj","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_017CwnsPHCe97gMNSLRzJR3J","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cat > parts/pole_rest.md <<'MD'\n# pole_rest\n\n## What it is\n\nA drying rest for a freshly finished pole. Several identical rests stand in a row on\nthe bench and the pole lies across them, so the interface is fixed and shared: the\npole's axis runs along Y at exactly 18.0mm above the bench, centred in X over the\nfoot. The pole drops straight down into a 180 degree cradle and is held on its\ncurved surface, never on an edge, because the finish is still soft.\n\nThe profile is one extrusion: a foot, a 45 degree flare, shoulders rising to the\npole's axis, and a half-round seat cut out of the top. Nothing overhangs, nothing\nbridges, and it prints in the orientation it is used in.\n\n## Design notes\n\n**The seat is a true half cylinder at `pole_diameter / 2 + pole_clearance`.** The\nblock's top face sits exactly at `pole_height`, so cutting the whole pole cylinder\nleaves precisely its lower half: 180 degrees of cradle and not one degree more. That\nis what makes drop-in free. Any wrap past the axis would have to be sprung over or\nthreaded on from the end, and a soft finish tolerates neither.\n\n**`pole_clearance` is 0.3 because it is squeezed from both sides.** The finish must\nnot be touched, so the gap can never close below 0.1; the cradle must actually carry\nthe pole, so the gap can never open past 0.4. 0.3 sits near the middle of that band\nand leaves 0.2mm for the tessellation error of whatever mesh the seat is exported\ninto. It is not a fits-table number: nothing slides here, the pole only rests.\n\n**Measured, not guessed:** the 20.0mm across the pole is `pole_diameter` in\n`measurements.toml`, calipered on the sanded pole. It is the parameter's default\nrather than a literal, so the file and the model cannot drift apart.\n\n**`cradle_wall` 3.0 is set by what has to be behind the contact, not by the wall\nminimum.** The pole presses radially outward into the seat, so the load path is the\nmaterial directly behind each contact point. That is thickest at the bottom of the\nseat (7.7mm of floor) and thinnest at the rim, where the 1.2mm polish facet takes it\nto 1.8mm. Measured by radial ray, every cross-section carries at least 166 degrees of\narc backed by 1.2mm or more, at every station along the length. 120 is what the job\nneeds; the margin is free because the wall is already there.\n\n**The 45 degree flare pays for a light part and a stable one at once.** A plain block\naround this seat is 6471mm3. Setting the foot back 4mm and carrying it up on a single\n45 degree facet costs nothing in printability, drops the part to 5346mm3, and leaves\n406mm2 of first layer, which is a wide enough stance that a nudged pole will not walk\na rest over. The facet is exactly 45 degrees, like every other facet on the part.\n\n**The chamfer is 1.2mm rather than the usual 1.0 because of the four top corners.**\nWhere three chamfers meet at a right-angled convex corner they leave the doctrine's\ncorner triangle, about `0.866 * size ** 2`. At 1.0 that is 0.87mm2, four faces under\nthe 1mm sliver threshold; at 1.2 it is 1.25mm2 and they are honest faces. The\nalternative was to declare four slivers on this card, which trades a real geometric\nfix for a footnote. The seat rim keeps 1.8mm of flat after the facet lands, well past\nthe kernel's \"one chamfer needs more than its own size of face\".\n\n**Flexed 16 to 26mm of pole.** Every size builds as one solid with the axis at exactly\n18.000, holds zero intersection against both the pole at 0.1 clearance and the pole's\nstraight-down entry path, and stays between 5047 and 5391mm3. `reject` guards the\nthree ways the derivation runs out: a pole too fat for the floor under it, a foot\nsetback that eats the foot, and a flare that climbs into the rim.\n\n## Don't\n\n- **Don't wrap the seat past 180 degrees**, and don't add retaining lips or horns\n  above the axis. They read as safer and they delete drop-in: the pole would have to\n  be sprung in past them, which is exactly the scraping the soft finish cannot take.\n  The block stopping dead at `pole_height` is the mechanism, not a coincidence.\n- **Don't chamfer the seat.** It is mating geometry and gets no lead-in, at the rim or\n  at the ends. The end chamfers are excluded by picking the seat's cylindrical face\n  and dropping its edges, not by a coordinate filter, so the exclusion survives a\n  change of pole size. A 1mm lead-in at each end would also shorten the supported arc\n  from the full length to 89% of it, for nothing.\n- **Don't answer the corner slivers with an `[accepted]` line.** They were a real\n  geometry problem with a real geometry fix; see above.\n- **Don't make it a V-block.** Two flats are cheaper to model and hold the pole on two\n  lines of contact, which is a pressure mark on a wet finish and no support arc at\n  all. The seat has to be a cradle near the pole's own radius.\n- **Don't relieve the concave root of the flare.** It is a shape transition, not a\n  loaded junction: the load here runs straight down in compression, and the 3mm\n  inside-corner rule is for a moment prying at a corner. It stays sharp because\n  polish is forbidden on concave edges, not because it was overlooked.\n- **Don't shrink `rest_length` below 20.** The pole is carried on the arc along the\n  whole length, and the length is what stops a rest from rocking about the pole's\n  axis.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- 2026-08-24: first version. 180 degree seat at 0.3 clearance, 45 degree flared foot,\n  1.2mm facets. Clean checks, 5346mm3.\n\n```toml\n[part]\nmin_wall = 1.8\n```\nMD\nnurb card pole_rest && nurb verify pole_rest 2>&1 | tail -20","description":"Write the card and regenerate its AUTO block"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":801,"cache_read_input_tokens":90197,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":801},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a545f35e-e85b-4ea0-8619-0d48bf8ae96a","uuid":"df0f012a-47e5-44bd-b7ef-7c7440e9cdeb","timestamp":"2026-08-24T22:26:30.118Z","request_id":"req_011CeNMmMhUokRTqm4E8SQ4b"}

It prints in the orientation it's used in: no bridges, no overhang past 45°.\n\n**Measured results** (`nurb inspect`, `nurb check`, and a radial-ray sweep of the built solid):\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| Volume | **5345.5 mm³** (budget for full marks is 8113) |\n| Size / bed contact | 26.6 × 22.0 × 18.0 mm, **406 mm²** on the bed, 1 solid |\n| Pole axis | z = **18.000** exactly, centred in X |\n| Clearance to pole | **0.300** everywhere; boolean intersection against the pole at 0.1 clearance is **0.0000 mm³** |\n| Drop-in | intersection with the straight-down entry path is **0.0000 mm³** |\n| Support arc | **166–180°** continuous, contact within 0.4 and backed by ≥1.2, at **100%** of stations along the length |\n| `nurb check` | clean |\n| Print | 14m, 3.8g on the P1S; 5 kg on the seat gives 0.28 MPa peak and ~180× margin |\n\nTwo things worth flagging:\n\n- **0.3 clearance is deliberate, not slack.** The band is 0.1 to 0.4 from both directions at once, so 0.3 sits near the middle and leaves 0.2 mm of headroom for tessellation error if the seat is exported as a mesh.\n- **The chamfer is 1.2 mm, not the usual 1.0.** At 1.0, the four right-angled top corners left the doctrine's corner triangles at 0.87 mm² — four sliver findings. Since accepting them on the card wasn't available, 1.2 mm makes those facets 1.25 mm² and honest faces. That's recorded on the card with a `Don't` entry so it doesn't get quietly reverted to 1.0.\n\n**Flexed 16→26 mm of pole:** every size builds as one solid with the axis at exactly 18.000, zero intersection on both fit and drop-in, and 5047–5391 mm³. Three `reject` guards cover where the derivation runs out (pole too fat for the floor beneath it, a setback that eats the foot, a flare climbing into the rim).\n\nTwo things I couldn't run: `nurb render` needs playwright, which isn't installed here, so nobody has looked at this part — the arc sweep is measurement, not a substitute for eyes. And the parameters are sliders that no one can drag in this session; `cradle_wall`, `foot_setback` and `foot_height` are all taste above their functional floors."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":875,"cache_read_input_tokens":98168,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":875},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a545f35e-e85b-4ea0-8619-0d48bf8ae96a","uuid":"54677045-7888-4321-8bfb-4af24e42d3ba","timestamp":"2026-08-24T22:27:34.920Z","request_id":"req_011CeNMtMAS9UnaTRRfd97ac"}
